**[00:16:59.000] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000049a)  
Good morning, everyone. This briefing of the Development Budget Coordination Committee, or the DBCC, is hereby called to order. Please rise for the singing of the national anthem and please remain standing for the opening prayer to be led by Vice Chairperson Johanne Bautista of the TRABAHO Party-list.

**[00:17:17.303] House ceremonial singers** (asr-000049b)  
[National anthem performed]

**[00:18:29.528] Rep. Johanne Monich G. Bautista** (asr-000049c)  
Let us put ourselves in the presence of Almighty God. Ever-living God, we bow our heads before You today with hearts full of gratitude and profound responsibility as we begin our budget briefing. Grant us, Lord, the wisdom, discernment, and steadfast dedication needed to craft a national budget that truly serves the Filipino people, a budget that drives economic recovery, creates sustainable and decent livelihoods, and uplifts every worker, family, and community across our archipelago. Lord God of creation, we also place before Your loving protection the Filipino people, especially as our nation faces the trials of inclement weather and natural calamities. We ask for Your merciful mantle of safety and security to shield every community, home, and vulnerable family. Please grant strength and resilience to our frontline workers, local leaders, and disaster response teams, and guide our hands to allocate the resources necessary to protect, prepare, and deliver swift aid to those in need. May everything we discuss and decide today and in the next few weeks be guided by integrity, compassion, and unwavering commitment to public service and the common good. Amen.

**[00:28:17.420] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000119)

**[00:35:43.040] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000212)  
Consistent with our mandate, we worry about inflation because it diminishes the purchasing power of Filipino families. Low inflation also supports sustainable growth. We are encouraged to report that

**[00:36:03.740] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000213)  
inflation has eased somewhat over the last 3 months. However, inflation remains well above

**[00:36:13.020] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000217)  
this is due largely to global supply shocks and their spillover effects on prices in the domestic

**[00:36:21.260] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000218)  
economy as you know oil price shocks are are not just about energy they are also about food prices

**[00:36:31.260] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000219)  
because oil is also the source of much of our fertilizer

**[00:36:35.180] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000220)  
to contain price pressures the bsp has responded by tightening monetary policy

**[00:36:44.860] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000221)  
twice this year these were carefully calibrated moves to help slow down inflation

**[00:36:54.540] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000222)  
anchor inflation expectations while recognizing the temporary weakness in growth

**[00:37:03.100] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000223)  
over the medium term we expect inflation to increase

**[00:37:05.980] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000224)  
gradually however risks remain tilted to the upside the monetary board will meet next week

**[00:37:17.100] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000225)  
to decide on the next policy action we look at all the evidence and we are prepared

**[00:37:24.700] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000226)  
to take further steps as necessary to ensure that inflation returns to target

**[00:37:33.420] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000227)  
i'm also glad to report

**[00:37:35.820] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000228)  
that the philippine banking system remains well provisioned and well capitalized

**[00:37:43.100] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000229)  
banks are also flush with liquidity they are well positioned to continue to support economic activity

**[00:37:52.780] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000230)  
our strong external position also provides an important buffer against global uncertainties

**[00:38:03.260] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000231)  
this macroeconomic backdrop

**[00:38:05.580] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000232)  
supports the assumptions behind the proposed national government budget for 2027.

**[00:38:13.500] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000233)  
with that let me turn the floor over to deputy governor zeno Abenoja who will provide a more

**[00:38:20.220] BSP Governor Eli M. Remolona Jr.** (asr-000234)

thank you governor i joined the governor in

**[00:38:32.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000236)  
the last minutes uh saying our thanks to our honorable speaker Speaker Bodjie Dy III our

**[00:38:43.260] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000237)  
honorable chair Congresswoman Mikaela Angela Suansing and our honorable representatives present

**[00:38:50.700] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000238)  
at today's briefing the bsp is a resource institution of the dbcc

**[00:38:57.260] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000239)  
and for this morning we will provide some context for the macroeconomic

**[00:38:57.980] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000240)  
this morning we will provide some context for the macroeconomic developments and assumptions

**[00:39:02.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000241)  
assumptions behind the proposed national government budget for the fiscal year 2027.

**[00:39:09.980] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000242)  
So let me start with some brief overview of what is happening in the economy.

**[00:39:16.780] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000243)  
What is the latest trend in terms of inflation? In 2025, we see here that lower food, particularly

**[00:39:26.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000244)  
rice and energy prices significantly eased the country's inflation. This continued in the first

**[00:39:35.100] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000245)  
two months of this year, 2026. However, the conflict in the Middle East since end February

**[00:39:42.060] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000246)  
2026 drove global oil prices higher, and this translated to significant spillover

**[00:39:49.660] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000247)  
to domestic inflation. Inflation rose from 2.4%

**[00:39:56.860] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000248)  
in February of this year to a recent peak at 7.2% in April. However, since then, it has gradually

**[00:40:07.180] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000249)  
eased to 6.2% by July. However, that number, that inflation number, continued to be well

**[00:40:17.260] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000250)  
above the target of 3%. The moderation that we have seen since the period May to July

**[00:40:26.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000251)  
in inflation reflects lower global oil prices and improve food supply conditions.

**[00:40:34.940] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000252)  
What was the response of the BSP?

**[00:40:38.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000253)  
As the Governor has mentioned, monetary policy shifted to a tightening stance with this rise in

**[00:40:45.820] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000254)  
inflation, and also the increase in the inflation outlook. The BSP decided to raise target policy rates,

**[00:40:56.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000255)  
from 4.25% by 25 basis points each in April and June 2026.

**[00:41:05.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000256)  
This brings the policy rate to 4.75%.

**[00:41:10.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000257)  
This calibrated move by the BSP helps anchor inflation expectations

**[00:41:17.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000258)  
and reduce what we call second round risks.

**[00:41:21.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000259)  
At the same time, this measured policy action supports growth.

**[00:41:28.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000260)  
The calibrated monetary policy action will complement fiscal measures

**[00:41:33.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000261)  
in supporting steady consumption and strengthening business sentiment.

**[00:41:38.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000262)  
In the coming months, in the coming quarters, amid the challenging external environment,

**[00:41:44.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000263)  
the BSP will remain data dependent.

**[00:41:47.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000264)  
The BSP will remain vigilant and prepared to take first steps

**[00:41:51.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000265)  
to further monetary actions to ensure that inflation returns

**[00:41:55.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000266)  
to the target of 3% over the medium term.

**[00:42:00.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000267)  
Where do we see inflation going this year and next year in 2027?

**[00:42:07.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000268)  
The inflation outlook has deteriorated amid the challenging external environment

**[00:42:14.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000269)  
and amid the faster than expected broadening of price pressure.

**[00:42:20.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000270)  
We can see this.

**[00:42:21.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000271)  
We can tell from what we call core inflation numbers.

**[00:42:25.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000272)  
Our analysis point to stronger second round effects

**[00:42:28.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000273)  
and a wider pass-through of earlier supply shocks

**[00:42:32.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000274)  
to the different components of the consumption basket.

**[00:42:37.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000275)  
In the monetary policy meeting last June,

**[00:42:40.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000276)  
the BSP sees headline inflation averaging at 6.4% in 2026,

**[00:42:48.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000277)  
4.5% in 2027,

**[00:42:50.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000278)  
and 4.5% in 2027.

**[00:42:54.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000279)  
And both numbers are above the inflation target of 3%.

**[00:43:00.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000280)  
However, by 2028, it is possible that inflation will be close to the target

**[00:43:05.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000281)  
at around 3.1%.

**[00:43:09.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000282)  
The BSP sees upside inflation risk,

**[00:43:13.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000283)  
and this could emanate largely from the higher global oil prices

**[00:43:18.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000284)  
and higher inflation expectations.

**[00:43:22.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000285)  
If you look at private sector inflation forecasts,

**[00:43:25.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000286)  
they could also see higher inflation in the near term.

**[00:43:29.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000287)  
But this could revert to near the 3% level by 2028 or 2029.

**[00:43:38.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000288)  
Now going to the banking system,

**[00:43:41.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000289)  
how is the banking system performing?

**[00:43:44.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000290)  
The banking system remains healthy.

**[00:43:48.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000291)  
The Philippine banking system is supported by strong fundamentals.

**[00:43:53.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000292)  
Banks sustain growth in assets and deposits

**[00:43:57.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000293)  
and continued profitability.

**[00:44:00.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000294)  
Capital and liquidity metrics remain well above international standards,

**[00:44:05.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000295)  
and non-performing loans are manageable and well provisioned.

**[00:44:10.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000296)  
Looking ahead, the banking system is well positioned

**[00:44:15.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000297)  
to support economic activity

**[00:44:17.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000298)  
and financial stability.

**[00:44:21.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000299)  
We also observed that more Filipinos

**[00:44:24.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000300)  
continue to participate in the formal financial system.

**[00:44:29.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000301)  
The chart on the left shows that digital payments

**[00:44:33.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000302)  
reached almost 65% of retail transactions last year.

**[00:44:39.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000303)  
This surpasses the targets of 50% to 54%.

**[00:44:45.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000304)  
And our data shows even more.

**[00:44:47.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000305)  
In the last few months,

**[00:44:48.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000306)  
we have seen an increase in the number of payments

**[00:44:51.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000307)  
that have been made through digital payments.

**[00:44:54.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000308)  
This is due to increasing use of payments

**[00:44:57.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000309)  
through Instapay, Pesonet, and person-to-merchant QRPH.

**[00:45:01.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000310)  
This highlights the continued momentum

**[00:45:04.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000311)  
on the adoption of electronic payments channels in the country.

**[00:45:08.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000312)  
Equally important,

**[00:45:10.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000313)  
the growing adoption of digital payments

**[00:45:13.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000314)  
also generates what are called network externalities.

**[00:45:17.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000315)  
Electronic payment channels increase

**[00:45:20.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000316)  
as more consumers, more merchants,

**[00:45:23.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000317)  
and more financial institutions participate

**[00:45:26.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000318)  
in this financial ecosystem.

**[00:45:31.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000319)  
On the right side,

**[00:45:33.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000320)  
we show the increasing number of basic deposit accounts,

**[00:45:37.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000321)  
and it has reached over 28 million as of March 2026,

**[00:45:43.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000322)  
with 177 banks offering basic deposits.

**[00:45:46.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000323)  
This is one of the ways

**[00:45:49.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000324)  
that we at the BSP promote financial inclusion.

**[00:45:54.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000325)  
We also welcome the decision of several banks

**[00:45:57.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000326)  
to reduce or waive interbank digital transaction fees.

**[00:46:03.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000327)  
This reduction or waiver of interbank digital transaction fees

**[00:46:08.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000328)  
makes it more affordable and accessible

**[00:46:12.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000329)  
for Filipino households and small businesses

**[00:46:15.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000330)  
to access the convenience of electronic fund transfers.

**[00:46:23.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000331)  
We also thank our legislators

**[00:46:25.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000332)  
for the passage of the Anti-Financial Accounts Coming Act.

**[00:46:29.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000333)  
This is indeed an important legislation

**[00:46:32.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000334)  
to continue to improve the consumer protection

**[00:46:35.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000335)  
through stronger safeguards for fraud

**[00:46:38.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000336)  
and faster complaint resolutions.

**[00:46:42.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000337)  
At the external front,

**[00:46:44.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000338)  
if you look at our external sector,

**[00:46:47.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000339)  
it continues to protect our economy

**[00:46:50.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000340)  
from the effects of the volatile external conditions.

**[00:46:54.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000341)  
Right now, we have a balance of payments deficit

**[00:46:57.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000342)  
of about 1.2% of GDP.

**[00:47:01.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000343)  
This is for last year,

**[00:47:03.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000344)  
and the current account narrowed

**[00:47:06.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000345)  
due to strong exports and remittances.

**[00:47:09.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000346)  
For 2026 and 2027,

**[00:47:12.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000347)  
the current account deficit is projected to slightly widen,

**[00:47:18.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000348)  
and this is consistent with the experience

**[00:47:20.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000349)  
of many emerging market economies

**[00:47:23.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000350)  
pursuing investment-led growth.

**[00:47:26.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000351)  
For the Philippines,

**[00:47:27.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000352)  
the wider deficit primarily reflects strong demand

**[00:47:31.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000353)  
for capital goods and other productive imports

**[00:47:34.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000354)  
needed to support infrastructure development

**[00:47:37.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000355)  
and expand productive activity.

**[00:47:40.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000356)  
How is this deficit being supported right now?

**[00:47:45.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000357)  
This highlights the importance

**[00:47:47.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000358)  
of structural foreign exchange inflows,

**[00:47:50.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000359)  
the remittances, the revenues from BPO's,

**[00:47:53.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000360)  
and also the inflows from foreign direct investments.

**[00:47:58.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000361)  
We are also maintaining enough buffers

**[00:48:03.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000362)  
in terms of international reserves.

**[00:48:05.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000363)  
As of now, our international reserve stands

**[00:48:08.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000364)  
at almost $105 billion.

**[00:48:10.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000365)  
That is as of end June 2026,

**[00:48:15.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000366)  
and it's equivalent to about seven months' worth

**[00:48:18.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000367)  
of imports of goods and services.

**[00:48:22.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000368)  
So this is the general macroeconomic landscape

**[00:48:27.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000369)  
that we are working with at this time.

**[00:48:31.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000370)  
And the key assumptions behind the proposed national budget

**[00:48:35.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000371)  
for fiscal year 2027

**[00:48:38.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000372)  
are shown in this particular slide.

**[00:48:40.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000373)  
In terms of inflation,

**[00:48:43.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000374)  
as mentioned earlier,

**[00:48:44.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000375)  
our latest inflation forecast

**[00:48:46.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000376)  
points to still elevated inflation for 2026

**[00:48:51.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000377)  
before easing in 2027.

**[00:48:54.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000378)  
For the national government budget purposes,

**[00:48:57.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000379)  
we assume that headline inflation

**[00:48:59.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000380)  
to be around 4% to 5% for 2027.

**[00:49:04.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000381)  
Oil prices remain volatile and highly uncertain,

**[00:49:09.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000382)  
largely dependent on geopolitical developments,

**[00:49:12.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000383)  
particularly in the Middle East.

**[00:49:14.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000384)  
Markets right now are pointing to some decline

**[00:49:17.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000385)  
in the price of Dubai crude oil

**[00:49:20.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000386)  
to about the range of $70 to $90 per barrel

**[00:49:24.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000387)  
on average in 2027.

**[00:49:28.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000388)  
Exchange rate will be broadly stable

**[00:49:31.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000389)  
against the US dollar,

**[00:49:32.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000390)  
reflecting external developments

**[00:49:34.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000391)  
and also at the same time some domestic headwinds.

**[00:49:38.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000392)  
On the interest rates,

**[00:49:40.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000393)  
particularly domestic interest rates,

**[00:49:42.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000394)  
the one-year T-bill rate will range around 5% to 6%

**[00:49:46.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000395)  
in 2026 and 2027,

**[00:49:49.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000396)  
and this reflects the country's fiscal condition

**[00:49:53.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000397)  
and the policy of continued consolidation.

**[00:49:56.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000398)  
In the international interest rates,

**[00:49:59.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000399)  
the six-month secured overnight financing rate,

**[00:50:03.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000400)  
which is the global short-term rate benchmark in US dollars,

**[00:50:07.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000401)  
is assumed to be within 3% to 4% in 2026 as well as in 2027.

**[00:50:15.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000402)  
This reflects expectations that the US Fed

**[00:50:18.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000403)  
may maintain a relatively cautious stance

**[00:50:21.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000404)  
in terms of monetary policy.

**[00:50:24.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000405)  
Finally, our exports of goods will grow modestly,

**[00:50:27.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000406)  
boosted by firm global demand for electronics and manufactures,

**[00:50:33.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000407)  
but constrained by the slow global economy

**[00:50:36.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000408)  
and increased global trade uncertainty.

**[00:50:40.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000409)  
Let me end with some remarks.

**[00:50:43.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000410)  
First, inflation outlook remains elevated,

**[00:50:47.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000411)  
requiring measured action to anchor inflation expectations

**[00:50:52.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000412)  
and contain broadening price pressures.

**[00:50:56.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000413)  
We have adequate external buffers

**[00:50:59.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000414)  
and a resilient, healthy banking system

**[00:51:02.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000415)  
to provide support to domestic economic activity.

**[00:51:06.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000416)  
Moving forward,

**[00:51:07.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000417)  
the BSP is prepared to take further monetary actions

**[00:51:11.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000418)  
if needed to ensure its primary mandate

**[00:51:14.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000419)  
of maintaining price stability

**[00:51:17.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000420)  
conducive to sustainable and durable growth.

**[00:51:21.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000421)  
We end here, Madam Chair.

**[00:51:24.380] BSP Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald R. Abenoja** (asr-000422)  
Again, thank you very much.

**[00:51:27.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000423)  
Thank you very much to the BSP family.

**[00:51:30.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000424)  
Thank you to Governor Eli Remolona Jr.

**[00:51:34.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000425)  
and Deputy Governor Zeno Ronald,

**[00:51:36.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000426)  
and Deputy Governor Abenoja.

**[00:51:37.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000427)  
Thank you very much.

**[00:51:38.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000428)  
Next, we move to the presentation

**[00:51:40.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000429)  
of the Department of Economy, Planning and Development,

**[00:51:43.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000430)  
or DEPDev,

**[00:51:44.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000431)  
headed by Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan.

**[00:51:47.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000432)  
Secretary Balisacan, kindly proceed po.

**[00:51:52.380] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000433)  
Thank you very much.

**[00:51:53.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000434)  
Thank you, Speaker Di III,

**[00:51:57.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000435)  
Chairperson Swan Singh,

**[00:51:59.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000436)  
and distinguished members of the Committee on Appropriations

**[00:52:03.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000437)  
for this opportunity

**[00:52:04.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000438)  
to discuss the state of the Philippine economy

**[00:52:06.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000439)  
and our economic directions moving forward.

**[00:52:11.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000440)  
My task this morning

**[00:52:13.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000441)  
is to provide the economic development context

**[00:52:16.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000442)  
for the proposed 2027 National Budget,

**[00:52:19.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000443)  
where the economy stands,

**[00:52:22.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000444)  
why growth has slowed sharply in recent quarters,

**[00:52:25.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000445)  
and what directions must guide

**[00:52:27.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000446)  
our policies and investments moving forward.

**[00:52:32.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000447)  
The 2027 budget must help us

**[00:52:35.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000448)  
protect the gains already achieved,

**[00:52:38.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000449)  
address critical implementations

**[00:52:40.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000450)  
and structural bottlenecks,

**[00:52:42.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000451)  
and restore the economy to a faster,

**[00:52:45.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000452)  
more durable, and inclusive growth path.

**[00:52:49.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000453)  
I will cover three areas.

**[00:52:52.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000454)  
First, our economic performance

**[00:52:54.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000455)  
and recent developments.

**[00:52:56.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000456)  
Second, the emerging risk

**[00:52:58.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000457)  
and persistent structural constraints.

**[00:53:01.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000458)  
And third, our outlook and priorities

**[00:53:04.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000459)  
for recovering growth

**[00:53:06.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000460)  
and laying the foundations for sustained development.

**[00:53:10.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000461)  
Let me begin with our economic performance.

**[00:53:14.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000462)  
Over the past several years,

**[00:53:17.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000463)  
the Philippine economy achieved important gains

**[00:53:21.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000464)  
in growth, employment, and poverty reduction.

**[00:53:26.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000465)  
More recent developments, however,

**[00:53:28.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000466)  
show a sharp loss of momentum.

**[00:53:32.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000467)  
Taking the longer view,

**[00:53:34.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000468)  
the Philippine economy has become stronger.

**[00:53:37.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000469)  
From 2010 to 2025,

**[00:53:40.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000470)  
real GDP grew by an average of 5.1% a year,

**[00:53:45.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000471)  
faster than the 4.2% average

**[00:53:48.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000472)  
recorded during the preceding 15 years.

**[00:53:52.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000473)  
Following the pandemic,

**[00:53:56.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000474)  
economic growth averaged 5.8%

**[00:53:59.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000475)  
from 2022 to 2025.

**[00:54:02.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000476)  
However, momentum weakened sharply

**[00:54:06.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000477)  
beginning in the second half of 2025,

**[00:54:09.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000478)  
with growth slowing further to 2.6%

**[00:54:12.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000479)  
in the first half of this year.

**[00:54:14.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000480)  
This sharp deceleration defines our immediate challenge

**[00:54:19.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000481)  
to recover growth without losing

**[00:54:22.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000482)  
the important development gains already achieved.

**[00:54:25.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000483)  
That milestone is the Philippines' transition

**[00:54:29.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000484)  
to upper middle-income status

**[00:54:31.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000485)  
after nearly four decades

**[00:54:34.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000486)  
as a lower middle-income economy.

**[00:54:37.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000487)  
The country's gross national income per capita,

**[00:54:41.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000488)  
or GNI,

**[00:54:42.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000489)  
reached US$4,850 in 2025,

**[00:54:47.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000490)  
surpassing the World Bank's threshold

**[00:54:49.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000491)  
of US$4,636.

**[00:54:53.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000492)  
As the chart on the right shows,

**[00:54:56.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000493)  
the Philippines has also moved closer

**[00:54:59.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000494)  
to its Asian peers,

**[00:55:00.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000495)  
although considerable income gaps remain.

**[00:55:04.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000496)  
Reaching upper middle-income status

**[00:55:07.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000497)  
is an important achievement,

**[00:55:09.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000498)  
but it is or it is not an end in itself.

**[00:55:13.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000499)  
The greater challenge is to translate this progress

**[00:55:17.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000500)  
into better jobs, higher incomes,

**[00:55:20.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000501)  
and improved living standards for Filipinos.

**[00:55:23.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000502)  
To do so, we must first understand the factors

**[00:55:27.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000503)  
behind the recent loss of economic momentum.

**[00:55:30.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000504)  
The sharp slowdown largely reflected

**[00:55:35.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000505)  
weaker domestic demand,

**[00:55:37.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000506)  
as governance and corruption concerns

**[00:55:40.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000507)  
and the Middle East conflict weighed

**[00:55:43.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000508)  
on consumer and business confidence.

**[00:55:46.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000509)  
Domestic demand grew by only 1.5%

**[00:55:49.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000510)  
in the first half of 2026,

**[00:55:51.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000511)  
down from 5.7% in the same period last year.

**[00:55:56.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000512)  
Investment contracted by 6.5%,

**[00:55:59.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000513)  
driven largely by the sharp decline

**[00:56:02.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000514)  
in public construction.

**[00:56:04.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000515)  
Household consumption growth also eased to 2.9%,

**[00:56:08.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000516)  
amid elevated inflation and weaker confidence.

**[00:56:13.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000517)  
Exports provided some support,

**[00:56:16.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000518)  
growing by 10%.

**[00:56:18.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000519)  
On the production side, however,

**[00:56:21.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000520)  
services remained the principal source of growth,

**[00:56:24.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000521)  
while industry contracted and agriculture

**[00:56:28.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000522)  
made only a modest contribution.

**[00:56:31.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000523)  
The weakening of economic activity

**[00:56:34.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000524)  
has also begun to affect the labour market.

**[00:56:37.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000525)  
The post-pandemic expansion generated 4 million net jobs

**[00:56:42.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000526)  
between the first half of 2022 and the first half of 2026,

**[00:56:47.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000527)  
demonstrating how economic growth can create opportunities

**[00:56:51.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000528)  
and improve livelihoods.

**[00:56:54.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000529)  
However, as growth slowed,

**[00:56:57.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000530)  
total employment declined by 166,000 in the past year.

**[00:57:03.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000531)  
The unemployment rate also rose to 4.2% in June 2026,

**[00:57:08.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000532)  
from 3.1% a year earlier.

**[00:57:11.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000533)  
These recent developments underscore the urgency

**[00:57:15.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000534)  
of restoring economic momentum

**[00:57:17.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000535)  
and generating more and better quality jobs.

**[00:57:22.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000536)  
The benefits of recent economic growth extended beyond employment,

**[00:57:26.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000537)  
poverty declined substantially,

**[00:57:29.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000538)  
regardless of the poverty threshold or norm used.

**[00:57:33.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000539)  
Based on the country's official poverty lines

**[00:57:37.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000540)  
and the preliminary estimates for 2025,

**[00:57:40.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000541)  
poverty incidence,

**[00:57:42.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000542)  
or the proportion of the population deemed poor among Filipinos,

**[00:57:46.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000543)  
fell from 18.1% in 2021 to 9.7% in 2025.

**[00:57:54.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000544)  
This means that 8.8 million Filipinos

**[00:57:58.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000545)  
were lifted out of poverty over this period.

**[00:58:01.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000546)  
These gains reflect not only the recovery of incomes and employment,

**[00:58:07.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000547)  
but also the sustained implementation of social protection programs.

**[00:58:11.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000548)  
However, these hard-won gains remain vulnerable,

**[00:58:16.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000549)  
particularly to high inflation,

**[00:58:19.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000550)  
which disproportionately affects poor and low-income households.

**[00:58:24.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000551)  
As Governor Remolona just mentioned,

**[00:58:27.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000552)  
or discussed earlier,

**[00:58:29.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000553)  
inflation rose sharply beginning in March,

**[00:58:32.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000554)  
driven largely by the effects of the Middle East conflict

**[00:58:35.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000555)  
on global oil and commodity prices.

**[00:58:39.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000556)  
Headline inflation is slightly to 6.2% in July

**[00:58:43.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000557)  
from 6.4% in June,

**[00:58:47.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000558)  
but it remains well above the government's target range.

**[00:58:53.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000559)  
High inflation erodes household purchasing power,

**[00:58:57.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000560)  
disproportionately affecting poor and low-income families.

**[00:59:01.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000561)  
The burden is even heavier

**[00:59:03.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000562)  
when food prices rise faster than non-food prices,

**[00:59:08.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000563)  
as food accounts for a much larger share of total consumption spending

**[00:59:13.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000564)  
among low-income families than among high-income families.

**[00:59:18.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000565)  
If inflation remains elevated,

**[00:59:20.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000566)  
it could slow or even reverse

**[00:59:22.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000567)  
our recent gains in poverty reduction.

**[00:59:25.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000568)  
Protecting these gains while recovering economic growth

**[00:59:28.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000569)  
will require us to address both emerging risk

**[00:59:32.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000570)  
and the economy's persistent structural constraints.

**[00:59:36.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000571)  
Beyond the immediate concerns of slower growth and elevated inflation,

**[00:59:42.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000572)  
persistent structural constraints continue to limit

**[00:59:45.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000573)  
the economy's productive capacity and resilience.

**[00:59:49.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000574)  
Let me now turn to these constraints.

**[00:59:52.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000575)  
A central structural challenge

**[00:59:55.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000576)  
is the economy's heavy reliance on household consumption

**[00:59:58.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000577)  
under the

**[01:00:00.000] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000578)  
side and services on the production side of the economy. Consumption remains the

**[01:00:06.660] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000579)  
principal driver of economic growth, while the contributions of fixed

**[01:00:11.520] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000580)  
investment exports, net of imports, have been relatively limited. Similarly,

**[01:00:18.840] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000581)  
services account for most of the expansion, while agriculture and industry

**[01:00:24.120] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000582)  
have contributed much less. The experience of successful economies shows

**[01:00:31.360] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000583)  
that sustained progress requires mobilizing other sources of growth,

**[01:00:35.540] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000584)  
notably investment, exports, agriculture, and industry. We must therefore broaden

**[01:00:43.300] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000585)  
the source of growth by strengthening investment and exports, while

**[01:00:47.380] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000586)  
revitalizing agriculture and industry. The urgency of this task is evident

**[01:00:54.100] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000587)  
from our performance relative to our ASEAN peers. As the charts show, the

**[01:01:02.480] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000588)  
Philippines continues to trail several neighboring economies in both

**[01:01:07.700] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000589)  
fixed investment and exports, despite improvements in recent years.

**[01:01:14.020] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000590)  
Investment expands productive capacity, facilitates the adoption of new

**[01:01:19.480] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000591)  
technologies, and creates better jobs.

**[01:01:24.100] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000592)  
Exports enable domestic firms to reach larger markets, achieve economies of

**[01:01:29.280] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000593)  
scale, and become more competitive. Mobilizing greater investment and

**[01:01:35.160] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000594)  
improving export competitiveness will depend, critically, on high quality and

**[01:01:40.700] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000595)  
reliable infrastructure. The government has substantially increased

**[01:01:46.620] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000596)  
infrastructure spending in recent years. Yet, this higher level of expenditure has

**[01:01:53.280] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000597)  
not translated for the better. The government has substantially increased infrastructure spending in recent years. Yet, this higher level of expenditure has not translated

**[01:01:54.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000598)  
fully into stronger infrastructure competitiveness and the Philippines continues to trail several ASEAN peers.

**[01:01:59.340] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000599)  
fully into stronger infrastructure competitiveness and the Philippines continues to trail several ASEAN peers.

**[01:02:02.940] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000600)  
This gap shows that the level of spending alone is not enough. We must also

**[01:02:09.720] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000601)  
improve project selection, appraisal, procurement, implementation, and

**[01:02:15.280] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000602)  
maintenance to ensure that public resources deliver high-quality

**[01:02:19.420] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000603)  
infrastructure on time and at reasonable cost.

**[01:02:24.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000604)  
Closing our infrastructure gaps will reduce production and logistic costs, connect people and markets, crowd in private investment, and strengthen the country's competitiveness.

**[01:02:39.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000605)  
Beyond physical infrastructure, faster growth also requires greater innovation and stronger productivity.

**[01:02:48.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000606)  
The Philippines has made encouraging progress in innovation, as reflected in its improved ranking in the Global Innovation Index.

**[01:02:59.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000607)  
However, these gains have yet to translate into stronger overall productivity, that is, how efficiently the economy combines its workers, capital, technology, and other resources to produce goods and services.

**[01:03:15.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000608)  
As the chart on the right shows,

**[01:03:18.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000609)  
our productivity performance remains weak relative to several of our regional peers.

**[01:03:25.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000610)  
Innovation contributes to development only when new knowledge and technologies are widely adapted by firms, farms, and workers,

**[01:03:34.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000611)  
and translated into more efficient production, better products, and higher value activities.

**[01:03:42.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000612)  
We must therefore strengthen the links among research, skills development, technology adoption,

**[01:03:48.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000613)  
commercialization, and production.

**[01:03:52.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000614)  
The weaknesses we have discussed do not arise from a single source.

**[01:03:57.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000615)  
They reflect persistent and interconnected constraints across markets and institutions.

**[01:04:04.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000616)  
These include infrastructure and human capital gaps, regulatory and administrative burdens, limited access to finance and technology,

**[01:04:12.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000617)  
weak competition in key sectors, and continuing governance concerns.

**[01:04:18.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000618)  
Together, these constraints raise the cost of doing business, discourage investment and innovation,

**[01:04:25.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000619)  
and limit the ability of firms and workers to move into more productive and higher-value activities.

**[01:04:33.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000620)  
Addressing them requires coordinated action across government in close partnership with Congress, the private sector, and other stakeholders.

**[01:04:45.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000621)  
Having identified these challenges,

**[01:04:47.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000622)  
I now turn to our growth targets and the priorities for moving the economy forward.

**[01:04:54.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000623)  
Our directions remain anchored in the Philippine Development Plan.

**[01:05:00.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000624)  
Over the Administration's final two years, the emphasis is on accelerating execution,

**[01:05:06.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000625)  
resolving implementation bottlenecks, and translating reforms into concrete results.

**[01:05:14.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000626)  
Given the sharp slowdown in recent years,

**[01:05:16.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000627)  
economic growth is projected at 3.5 to 4.5 percent in 2025.

**[01:05:25.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000628)  
With faster implementation of public programs and infrastructure projects,

**[01:05:31.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000629)  
improved domestic supply conditions,

**[01:05:34.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000630)  
and restored consumer and investor confidence,

**[01:05:37.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000631)  
growth is expected to recover to 5 to 6 percent in the next two years.

**[01:05:43.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000632)  
Inflation is expected to recover to 5 to 6 percent in the next two years.

**[01:05:45.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000633)  
Inflation is expected to remain elevated in 2026, as shown earlier by BSP,

**[01:05:51.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000634)  
eased to 4 to 5 percent in 2027,

**[01:05:54.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000635)  
and returned to the government's targets of 2 to 4 percent target range by 2028.

**[01:06:02.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000636)  
Meanwhile, employment conditions are expected to improve as economic growth recovers,

**[01:06:08.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000637)  
keeping unemployment within the government's target range.

**[01:06:12.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000638)  
Achieving these outcomes will require disciplined and coordinated execution across government,

**[01:06:19.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000639)  
along with the timely implementation of reforms that encourage private investment

**[01:06:24.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000640)  
and expand the economy's productive capacity.

**[01:06:27.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000641)  
These targets are attainable,

**[01:06:30.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000642)  
but they remain subject to significant domestic and external risk.

**[01:06:36.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000643)  
Achieving our growth, inflation, and employment targets

**[01:06:40.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000644)  
will require us to manage significant domestic and external risk.

**[01:06:45.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000645)  
Foremost among the domestic risks are further delays in the execution of public investments,

**[01:06:51.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000646)  
resulting in continued underspending,

**[01:06:53.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000647)  
as well as continuing governance concerns

**[01:06:56.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000648)  
that could further weaken consumer and investor confidence.

**[01:07:01.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000649)  
Severe weather disturbances could also constrain food production

**[01:07:05.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000650)  
and intensify inflationary pressures.

**[01:07:10.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000651)  
Externally, the prolonged Middle East conflict

**[01:07:13.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000652)  
could keep global oil and commodity prices elevated,

**[01:07:17.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000653)  
while geopolitical and trade tensions may weaken global demand

**[01:07:21.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000654)  
and disrupt supply chains.

**[01:07:25.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000655)  
These risks reinforce the need for timely and coordinated policies and execution

**[01:07:31.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000656)  
that address immediate pressures while strengthening the economy's productive capacity.

**[01:07:38.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000657)  
In the near term,

**[01:07:39.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000658)  
our priorities are to protect vulnerable households,

**[01:07:44.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000659)  
accelerate public investments,

**[01:07:46.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000660)  
and restore confidence.

**[01:07:48.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000661)  
First, we will caution poor and low-income families

**[01:07:51.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000662)  
from elevated food prices and supply disruptions

**[01:07:55.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000663)  
through targeted and timely assistance,

**[01:07:58.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000664)  
while strengthening domestic food supply and maintaining adequate buffers.

**[01:08:03.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000665)  
Second, implementing agencies will accelerate high-impact infrastructure projects

**[01:08:09.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000666)  
through catch-up plans with clear milestones and accountability measures.

**[01:08:15.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000667)  
This is essential to reverse underspending

**[01:08:18.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000668)  
and restore public investment as a driver of growth.

**[01:08:22.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000669)  
Third, we need to address governance and corruption concerns

**[01:08:26.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000670)  
by strengthening transparency and accountability,

**[01:08:29.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000671)  
improving monitoring,

**[01:08:31.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000672)  
and ensuring the efficient use of public resources.

**[01:08:35.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000673)  
These measures are critical to rebuilding consumer,

**[01:08:38.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000674)  
business, and investor confidence.

**[01:08:43.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000675)  
Alongside these measures,

**[01:08:44.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000676)  
our immediate interventions,

**[01:08:46.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000677)  
we must sustain investments that expand the economy's productive capacity

**[01:08:50.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000678)  
over the short to medium term.

**[01:08:54.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000679)  
Over the short to medium term,

**[01:08:55.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000680)  
limited public resources must be directed toward high-impact,

**[01:09:00.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000681)  
implementation-ready investments.

**[01:09:03.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000682)  
Priorities include addressing learning poverty and classroom shortages,

**[01:09:07.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000683)  
equipping workers with future-ready skills,

**[01:09:10.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000684)  
strengthening primary health care,

**[01:09:13.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000685)  
particularly in underserved areas,

**[01:09:16.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000686)  
raising farm productivity through climate-resilient technologies

**[01:09:20.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000687)  
and better post-harvest infrastructure,

**[01:09:23.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000688)  
and accelerating transport, logistics, energy,

**[01:09:27.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000689)  
and digital infrastructure

**[01:09:29.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000690)  
through the Build Better More program and public-private partnerships.

**[01:09:34.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000691)  
These investments must be pursued

**[01:09:36.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000692)  
while safeguarding fiscal sustainability.

**[01:09:40.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000693)  
This requires stronger coordination of planning,

**[01:09:43.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000694)  
investment programming,

**[01:09:45.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000695)  
budgeting, and execution of infrastructure and social programs,

**[01:09:49.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000696)  
improved spending efficiency,

**[01:09:51.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000697)  
modernized tax and customs administration,

**[01:09:55.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000698)  
and revenue measures that do not erode the government's fiscal position.

**[01:10:02.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000699)  
Beyond addressing immediate challenges,

**[01:10:05.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000700)  
sustained and structural reforms will be needed to raise productivity,

**[01:10:09.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000701)  
strengthen competitiveness,

**[01:10:11.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000702)  
and expand the economy's long-term growth potential.

**[01:10:15.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000703)  
Key priorities include ensuring more reliable and affordable energy

**[01:10:20.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000704)  
by expanding renewable generation,

**[01:10:22.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000705)  
modernizing the power grid,

**[01:10:25.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000706)  
streamlining net metering and permitting processes,

**[01:10:28.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000707)  
and strengthening competition in the electricity market.

**[01:10:33.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000708)  
Domestic production

**[01:10:34.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000709)  
and external competitiveness must likewise be strengthened

**[01:10:38.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000710)  
by moving into higher value segments of global value chains,

**[01:10:42.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000711)  
fostering stronger linkages between multinational and domestic firms,

**[01:10:47.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000712)  
sustaining the country's advantage in high-value services,

**[01:10:51.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000713)  
and expanding market access for Philippine exports.

**[01:10:55.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000714)  
Deeper financial and capital markets will also be essential

**[01:10:59.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000715)  
to mobilize long-term financing for infrastructure

**[01:11:02.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000716)  
and productive enterprises.

**[01:11:04.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000717)  
Legislation will be indispensable to advancing this reform agenda.

**[01:11:11.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000718)  
The priority measures span four broad areas.

**[01:11:16.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000719)  
Governance and anti-corruption reforms include measures on political dynasties,

**[01:11:22.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000720)  
the party-less system,

**[01:11:24.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000721)  
freedom of information,

**[01:11:26.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000722)  
bank secrecy law,

**[01:11:28.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000723)  
and public spending or budgeting.

**[01:11:30.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000724)  
Economic growth and resilience measures

**[01:11:33.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000725)  
cover tax reforms and amendments affecting the coconut and fisheries sectors.

**[01:11:40.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000726)  
Energy priorities include amendments to EPIRA

**[01:11:43.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000727)  
and legislation on waste to energy.

**[01:11:48.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000728)  
Measures on education, health, and social protection

**[01:11:51.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000729)  
seek to improve nutrition, accelerate classroom production,

**[01:11:55.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000730)  
expand access to education,

**[01:11:57.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000731)  
and strengthen assistance for vulnerable Filipinos.

**[01:12:01.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000732)  
Congress' continued partnership will be critical to advancing these reforms,

**[01:12:07.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000733)  
sustaining growth, and improving Filipino lives.

**[01:12:11.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000734)  
As we enter the final years of the administration,

**[01:12:16.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000735)  
the objective is to turn reforms into results.

**[01:12:20.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000736)  
We will consolidate our gains,

**[01:12:22.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000737)  
address critical implementation bottlenecks,

**[01:12:25.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000738)  
and advance strategic measures

**[01:12:28.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000739)  
to deliver people-centered growth

**[01:12:30.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000740)  
and a more inclusive and resilient future.

**[01:12:33.080] DEPDev Secretary Arsenio M. Balisacan** (asr-000741)  
Thank you.

**[01:12:38.080] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000742)  
Thank you very much, Secretary Balisacan.

**[01:12:41.080] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000743)  
We'll now move to the presentation from the Department of Finance.

**[01:12:45.080] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000744)  
May I now recognize the Honorable Secretary Frederick Go

**[01:12:49.080] Rep. Mikaela Angela B. Suansing** (asr-000745)  
for the presentation of the DOF family.

**[01:12:52.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000746)  
Good morning.

**[01:12:53.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000747)  
The honorable speaker, Bodjie Dy III,

**[01:12:56.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000748)  
Chair Mika Suansing,

**[01:12:58.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000749)  
Senior Vice Chair Abed Garcia,

**[01:13:00.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000750)  
Majority Floor Leader Marcos,

**[01:13:03.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000751)  
all the honorable members of this committee,

**[01:13:06.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000752)  
thank you for this opportunity to brief the House of Representatives

**[01:13:10.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000753)  
on the national government's fiscal position.

**[01:13:14.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000754)  
Our fiscal position today has been shaped by key economic developments,

**[01:13:19.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000755)  
from the circumstances this administration inherited

**[01:13:23.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000756)  
to the global conditions that have influenced our fiscal path since then.

**[01:13:28.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000757)  
Allow me to discuss briefly,

**[01:13:30.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000758)  
to provide context to where we are today

**[01:13:33.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000759)  
and where we are headed.

**[01:13:36.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000760)  
When the Marcos Jr. administration assumed office in 2022,

**[01:13:41.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000761)  
it inherited a massive 12.79 trillion debt,

**[01:13:47.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000762)  
the bulk of which was incurred from pandemic spending.

**[01:13:51.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000763)  
So when the economy began to recover,

**[01:13:54.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000764)  
the challenge before the administration was clear.

**[01:13:57.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000765)  
To rebuild fiscal space,

**[01:13:59.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000766)  
the government began to invest in the economy

**[01:14:01.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000767)  
while continuing to support economic recovery

**[01:14:03.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000768)  
and investing in the country's long-term growth.

**[01:14:06.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000769)  
As a response,

**[01:14:08.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000770)  
we crafted the country's medium-term fiscal framework in 2022.

**[01:14:15.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000771)  
The government's blueprint for gradually reducing our deficit and debt

**[01:14:20.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000772)  
while creating more jobs,

**[01:14:22.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000773)  
increasing people's incomes,

**[01:14:24.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000774)  
and reducing poverty.

**[01:14:26.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000775)  
It set us on a path of gradual and sustained fiscal consolidation

**[01:14:31.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000776)  
to restore fiscal space

**[01:14:33.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000777)  
while continuing to invest in the foundations of growth.

**[01:14:36.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000778)  
But our recovery has not happened in an easy environment.

**[01:14:41.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000779)  
Over the years,

**[01:14:42.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000780)  
external pressures and global uncertainties

**[01:14:44.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000781)  
have disrupted supply chains and pushed inflation up,

**[01:14:48.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000782)  
affecting the pace of growth.

**[01:14:51.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000783)  
In this context,

**[01:14:52.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000784)  
we had to recalibrate our growth and fiscal targets

**[01:14:55.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000785)  
to keep them realistic and responsive

**[01:14:58.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000786)  
to evolving economic conditions.

**[01:15:00.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000787)  
But the direction of our fiscal policy

**[01:15:03.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000788)  
has remained consistent.

**[01:15:06.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000789)  
The administration has remained committed

**[01:15:09.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000790)  
to the path of fiscal consolidation

**[01:15:11.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000791)  
with the goal of bringing down

**[01:15:13.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000792)  
the deficit to GDP ratio

**[01:15:16.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000793)  
from 5.4% this year

**[01:15:19.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000794)  
to just 3.5% by 2030

**[01:15:22.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000795)  
while supporting growth.

**[01:15:25.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000796)  
To stay the course amid growing political tensions

**[01:15:28.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000797)  
and the need to fund the government's priority programs

**[01:15:31.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000798)  
without relying on new taxes,

**[01:15:33.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000799)  
we strengthened revenues.

**[01:15:36.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000800)  
Total revenues grew double digits

**[01:15:39.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000801)  
at an annual average of 10.5%

**[01:15:42.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000802)  
over the last four years.

**[01:15:45.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000803)  
As tax revenues likewise increased

**[01:15:48.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000804)  
by an average of 10.47%

**[01:15:51.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000805)  
over the same period.

**[01:15:53.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000806)  
And finally,

**[01:15:54.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000807)  
for the first time in history,

**[01:15:57.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000808)  
in 2025,

**[01:15:59.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000809)  
the BIR surpassed 3 trillion pesos

**[01:16:02.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000810)  
in tax collections.

**[01:16:05.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000811)  
And we continue to stay the course

**[01:16:08.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000812)  
to meet our fiscal program for the year.

**[01:16:11.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000813)  
As of June 2026,

**[01:16:13.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000814)  
we have reached 49.7%

**[01:16:16.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000815)  
of our full-year target.

**[01:16:19.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000816)  
Despite global shocks and external pressures,

**[01:16:22.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000817)  
tax collections reached 2.14 trillion pesos

**[01:16:26.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000818)  
in the first half of the year,

**[01:16:28.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000819)  
5.4% higher than the same period last year.

**[01:16:33.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000820)  
The robust revenue performance

**[01:16:37.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000821)  
at 16.6% of GDP

**[01:16:40.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000822)  
is among the highest in Southeast Asia.

**[01:16:44.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000823)  
This reflects the continued efforts

**[01:16:48.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000824)  
of the BIR and BOC

**[01:16:50.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000825)  
to strengthen collection,

**[01:16:52.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000826)  
improve compliance,

**[01:16:54.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000827)  
and plug leakages.

**[01:16:56.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000828)  
As you can see from the slide,

**[01:16:58.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000829)  
the Philippines at 16.6%

**[01:17:01.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000830)  
revenue effort is better than Malaysia,

**[01:17:04.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000831)  
Singapore, Thailand, and Indonesia.

**[01:17:07.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000832)  
The Bureau of Customs,

**[01:17:10.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000833)  
in particular this year,

**[01:17:12.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000834)  
is on track to reach the 1 trillion peso mark

**[01:17:15.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000835)  
in collections.

**[01:17:17.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000836)  
With the BIR and BOC's

**[01:17:20.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000837)  
continued strong performance,

**[01:17:22.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000838)  
we expect total national government tax revenues

**[01:17:25.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000839)  
to reach 4.4 trillion pesos

**[01:17:28.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000840)  
by the end of 2026.

**[01:17:31.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000841)  
As the country's needs continue to grow,

**[01:17:36.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000842)  
we are also mobilizing additional sources of revenues

**[01:17:39.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000843)  
to help fund government priorities.

**[01:17:42.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000844)  
One important source is the privatization

**[01:17:45.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000845)  
of idle or non-core government assets.

**[01:17:48.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000846)  
We privatized

**[01:17:49.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000847)  
the 797-megawatt Kaliraya-Botocan-Kalayaan

**[01:17:54.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000848)  
hydroelectric power plant in 2025

**[01:17:57.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000849)  
with 36.27 billion pesos

**[01:18:00.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000850)  
in sale proceeds accruing in 2026.

**[01:18:05.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000851)  
These proceeds will be included

**[01:18:07.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000852)  
in the calculation of PESOM's

**[01:18:09.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000853)  
dividend remittance

**[01:18:10.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000854)  
to the national government in 2027.

**[01:18:14.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000855)  
In July 2026,

**[01:18:16.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000856)  
the DOF,

**[01:18:17.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000857)  
through the Privatization

**[01:18:18.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000858)  
and Management Office,

**[01:18:20.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000859)  
also successfully completed

**[01:18:21.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000860)  
the 1 billion peso sale

**[01:18:23.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000861)  
of government-owned properties

**[01:18:25.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000862)  
at the atrium of Makati,

**[01:18:27.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000863)  
covering 24 condominium units.

**[01:18:31.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000864)  
These transactions demonstrate

**[01:18:33.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000865)  
how we are unlocking value

**[01:18:35.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000866)  
from government assets

**[01:18:36.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000867)  
and ensuring that they support

**[01:18:38.080] DOF Secretary Frederick D. Go** (asr-000868)  
the government's fiscal requirements.
